Problem

View Problem Statement 
You are an expert Python programmer. You will be given a question (problem specification) and will generate a correct Python program that matches the specification and passes all tests.

Bob recently read about bitwise operations used in computers: AND, OR and XOR. He have studied their properties and invented a new game.

Initially, Bob chooses integer m, bit depth of the game, which means that all numbers in the game will consist of m bits. Then he asks Peter to choose some m-bit number. After that, Bob computes the values of n variables. Each variable is assigned either a constant m-bit number or result of bitwise operation. Operands of the operation may be either variables defined before, or the number, chosen by Peter. After that, Peter's score equals to the sum of all variable values.

Bob wants to know, what number Peter needs to choose to get the minimum possible score, and what number he needs to choose to get the maximum possible score. In both cases, if there are several ways to get the same score, find the minimum number, which he can choose.


-----Input-----

The first line contains two integers n and m, the number of variables and bit depth, respectively (1 ≤ n ≤ 5000; 1 ≤ m ≤ 1000). 

The following n lines contain descriptions of the variables. Each line describes exactly one variable. Description has the following format: name of a new variable, space, sign ":=", space, followed by one of:  Binary number of exactly m bits.  The first operand, space, bitwise operation ("AND", "OR" or "XOR"), space, the second operand. Each operand is either the name of variable defined before or symbol '?', indicating the number chosen by Peter.  

Variable names are strings consisting of lowercase Latin letters with length at most 10. All variable names are different.


-----Output-----

In the first line output the minimum number that should be chosen by Peter, to make the sum of all variable values minimum possible, in the second line output the minimum number that should be chosen by Peter, to make the sum of all variable values maximum possible. Both numbers should be printed as m-bit binary numbers.


-----Examples-----
Input
3 3
a := 101
b := 011
c := ? XOR b

Output
011
100

Input
5 1
a := 1
bb := 0
cx := ? OR a
d := ? XOR ?
e := d AND bb

Output
0
0



-----Note-----

In the first sample if Peter chooses a number 011_2, then a = 101_2, b = 011_2, c = 000_2, the sum of their values is 8. If he chooses the number 100_2, then a = 101_2, b = 011_2, c = 111_2, the sum of their values is 15.

For the second test, the minimum and maximum sum of variables a, bb, cx, d and e is 2, and this sum doesn't depend on the number chosen by Peter, so the minimum Peter can choose is 0.### Format: Read the inputs from stdin solve the problem and write the answer to stdout (do not directly test on the sample inputs). Enclose your code within delimiters as follows. Ensure that when the python program runs, it reads the inputs, runs the algorithm and writes output to STDOUT.

I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We have `n` variables and `m` bit depth
2. Peter chooses an m-bit number (represented by '?')
3. Each variable is assigned either:
   - A constant m-bit number, or
   - A bitwise operation (AND, OR, XOR) between existing variables or Peter's number
4. Peter's score is the sum of all variable values
5. We need to find:
   - The minimum number Peter should choose to minimize the total score
   - The minimum number Peter should choose to maximize the total score

## Key Insights

Since we're dealing with bitwise operations, we can analyze each bit position independently. This is because bitwise operations are independent across different bit positions.

For each bit position (0 to m-1), we can compute:
- What the contribution of that bit to the total score would be, depending on whether Peter chooses 0 or 1 for that position.

## Solution Approach

1. Parse the input to understand the dependencies
2. For each bit position, determine how the value at that position affects the total sum
3. Since operations are independent per bit, we can solve each bit position separately
4. For each bit position, calculate the contribution when Peter chooses 0 vs 1
5. For minimum score: choose 0 if it gives lower contribution, else 1 (and prefer smaller number in case of tie)
6. For maximum score: choose 1 if it gives higher contribution, else 0 (and prefer smaller number in case of tie)

## Step-by-Step Implementation

```python
def solve():
    n, m = map(int, input().split())
    
    # Parse the variable definitions
    variables = {}
    
    for _ in range(n):
        line = input().strip()
        parts = line.split(" := ")
        var_name = parts[0]
        expr = parts[1]
        
        # Parse the expression
        if expr[0] == '"':  # It's a constant
            # Extract the m-bit binary number
            constant = expr[1:-1]
        else:
            # It's an operation
            # Find the operation
            op_start = expr.find(" ")
            first_operand = expr[:op_start]
            operation = expr[op_start+1:op_start+4]  # "AND", "OR", "XOR"
            second_operand = expr[op_start+5:]
            
            # Replace '?' with the variable name
            first_operand = first_operand.replace('?', 'p')
            second_operand = second_operand.replace('?', 'p')
            
            # For now, just store the operation
            variables[var_name] = (first_operand, operation, second_operand)
    
    # For each bit position, we'll determine the contribution
    min_result = []
    max_result = []
    
    # We need to evaluate all variables for each bit position
    # For each bit position i, we'll compute the value of each variable when Peter's bit is 0 or 1
    
    for bit_pos in range(m):
        # For each possible value of Peter's bit at position bit_pos (0 or 1)
        # Calculate the total sum of all variable values
        def get_sum(p_bit):
            # Create a dictionary to store values of variables for this bit position
            var_values = {}
            
            # Initialize all variables with their values at this bit position
            for var_name in variables:
                # Get the right-hand side expression
                if var_name in variables:
                    first_operand, op, second_operand = variables[var_name]
                    
                    # Evaluate first operand
                    val1 = 0
                    if first_operand == 'p':
                        val1 = p_bit
                    else:
                        # Assume first_operand is a variable
                        if first_operand in var_values:
                            val1 = var_values[first_operand]
                        else:
                            # This should not happen - we need to process in order
                            pass
                    
                    # Evaluate second operand
                    val2 = 0
                    if second_operand == 'p':
                        val2 = p_bit
                    else:
                        if second_operand in var_values:
                            val2 = var_values[second_operand]
                        else:
                            pass
                    
                    # Apply operation
                    if op == "AND":
                        result = val1 & val2
                    elif op == "OR":
                        result = val1 | val2
                    elif op == "XOR":
                        result = val1 ^ val2
                    
                    var_values[var_name] = result
            
            # Sum up all variable values
            total = 0
            for var_name in var_values:
                total += var_values[var_name]
            
            return total
        
        # Calculate sum when Peter's bit is 0
        sum_0 = get_sum(0)
        # Calculate sum when Peter's bit is 1  
        sum_1 = get_sum(1)
        
        # For minimum: choose 0 if sum_0 <= sum_1, else choose 1
        # For maximum: choose 1 if sum_1 >= sum_0, else choose 0
        
        if sum_0 <= sum_1:
            min_result.append('0')
            if sum_0 == sum_1:
                # In case of tie, prefer smaller number (0)
                max_result.append('0')
            else:
                max_result.append('1')
        else:
            min_result.append('1')
            max_result.append('1')
    
    # Convert to m-bit binary numbers
    min_num = ''.join(min_result)
    max_num = ''.join(max_result)
    
    print(min_num)
    print(max_num)

solve()
```

```python
                    result = val1 | val2
                elif op == "XOR":
                    result = val1 ^ val2
                
                var_values[var_name] = result
```

I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We have `n` variables and `m` bit depth
2. Peter chooses an m-bit number `?` that will be used as input to various operations
3. Each variable is defined either as a constant m-bit number or as a result of bitwise operations (AND, OR, XOR) between previously defined variables or Peter's number
4. We need to find the number Peter should choose to minimize and maximize the total sum of all variable values
5. If multiple choices give the same score, we return the minimum number

## Key Insights

1. The operations are bitwise, so we can analyze each bit position independently due to the linearity of bitwise operations
2. For each bit position (0 to m-1), we can determine whether setting that bit in Peter's number increases, decreases, or doesn't change the total sum
3. Since we want to minimize or maximize the total sum independently in each bit position, we can solve each bit position separately
4. For the minimum sum: for each bit position, we choose 0 if it decreases the total sum, otherwise 1
5. For the maximum sum: for each bit position, we choose 1 if it increases the total sum, otherwise 0

## Algorithm

1. Parse the input to get variable definitions
2. For each bit position (0 to m-1), simulate the effect of setting that bit in Peter's number
3. For each bit position, calculate the contribution to the total sum when the bit is 0 vs when it's 1
4. For minimum sum: choose 0 if contributing less, otherwise 1
5. For maximum sum: choose 1 if contributing more, otherwise 0

## Step-by-step Solution

```python
def solve():
    n, m = map(int, input().split())
    
    # Store variable definitions
    variables = {}
    
    for _ in range(n):
        line = input().strip()
        parts = line.split(" := ")
        var_name = parts[0]
        expr = parts[1]
        
        # Parse the expression
        if expr[0] == '0' or expr[0] == '1' or expr[0] == '0' or expr[0] == '1':
            # It's a constant
            constant = expr
        else:
            # It's an operation
            op_parts = expr.split()
            if len(op_parts) == 3:
                first_operand = op_parts[0]
                op = op_parts[1]
                second_operand = op_parts[2]
            else:
                # Handle the case where it's just a constant
                first_operand = op_parts[0]
                op = op_parts[1]
                second_operand = op_parts[2]
            
            # For simplicity, we'll use the operation format only
            # In reality, we just need to extract the operation and operands
            if op == "AND" or op == "OR" or op == "XOR":
                # We'll determine the effect on each bit position
                pass
        
        variables[var_name] = expr
    
    # For each bit position, calculate the effect of setting that bit in Peter's number
    # We need to simulate the evaluation of each variable given a specific choice of Peter's number
    
    def evaluate_variable(var_name, peter_bit_values):
        # This function evaluates a variable given the bit values of Peter's number
        # For each variable, we need to evaluate its expression
        pass
    
    # Actually, let's think simpler: since operations are bitwise and independent,
    # we can analyze each bit position independently
    
    def get_bit_value_at_position(var_expr, peter_bit, position):
        # Given the expression and the bit value of Peter's number at this position,
        # return the bit value of this variable at this position
        # We need to parse the expression
        pass
    
    # Parse expressions to get a function that can evaluate each bit position
    expr_map = {}
    for var_name, expr in variables.items():
        expr_map[var_name] = expr
    
    # For each bit position, determine the effect
    min_peter = []
    max_peter = []
    
    for bit_pos in range(m):
        # We'll determine the contribution of setting this bit in Peter's number
        # to the total sum of all variables
        
        # Create a function that returns the value of a variable at this bit position
        def get_var_bit(var_name, peter_bit):
            # Simple evaluation - for now, just return 0
            # We need to parse the full expression
            pass
        
        # We need to simulate the evaluation of all variables at this bit position
        # given Peter's bit at this position is 0 or 1
        
        # First, parse the expressions
        var_bit_values = {}
        
        # For each variable, compute its bit value when Peter's bit is 0 and when it's 1
        # But since variables depend on each other, we need to process in order
        
        # Reset for this bit position
        values_at_0 = {}
        values_at_1 = {}
        
        # Process variables in order
        for var_name in variables.keys():
            expr = variables[var_name]
            # Parse the expression and compute its value at bit position
            # First check if it's a constant
            if expr[0] in ['0', '1']:
                # Constant m-bit number
                constant_bits = expr
                bit_value = int(constant_bits[bit_pos])
                values_at_0[var_name] = bit_value
                values_at_1[var_name] = bit_value
            else:
                # Operation expression
                op_parts = expr.split()
                first_operand = op_parts[0]
                op = op_parts[1]
                second_operand = op_parts[2]
                
                # Get the bit values of the operands
                first_val = 0
                second_val = 0
                
                if first_operand == '?':
                    first_val = 0  # When peter_bit = 0
                    first_val1 = 1  # When peter_bit = 1
                else:
                    # Get value from first_operand
                    if first_operand in values_at_0:
                        first_val = values_at_0[first_operand]
                    else:
                        # This should be handled properly
                        first_val = 0
                
                if second_operand == '?':
                    second_val = 0
                else:
                    if second_operand in values_at_0:
                        second_val = values_at_0[second_operand]
                    else:
                        second_val = 0
                
                # For bit position, we need to evaluate the operation
                # But we need the full expression for each peter bit value
                
        # Actually, let me simplify the approach:
        
        # For each bit position, we'll create a function to evaluate the value of a variable
        # given peter's bit at this position is 0 or 1
        
        # Process variables in order of definition
        values_at_0 = {}
        values_at_1 = {}
        
        for var_name in variables.keys():
            expr = variables[var_name]
            # Parse expression
            parts = expr.split()
            
            # Check if it's a constant
            if len(parts) == 1:
                # It's just a constant
                constant_bits = expr
                bit_value = int(constant_bits[bit_pos])
                values_at_0[var_name] = bit_value
                values_at_1[var_name] = bit_value
            else:
                # It's an operation
                first_operand = parts[0]
                op = parts[1]
                second_operand = parts[2]
                
                # Get values from first and second operands
                val1_0 = 0
                val1_1 = 0
                val2_0 = 0
                val2_1 = 0
                
                if first_operand == '?':
                    val1_0 = 0
                    val1_1 = 1
                else:
                    if first_operand in values_at_0:
                        val1_0 = values_at_0[first_operand]
                    else:
                        val1_0 = 0
                    if first_operand in values_at_1:
                        val1_1 = values_at_1[first_operand]
                    else:
                        val1_1 = 0
                        
                if second_operand == '?':
                    val2_0 = 0
                    val2_1 = 1
                else:
                    if second_operand in values_at_0:
                        val2_0 = values_at_0[second_operand]
                    else:
                        val2_0 = 0
                    if second_operand in values_at_1:
                        val2_1 = values_at_1[second_operand]
                    else:
                        val2_1 = 0
                
                # Apply operation
                if op == "AND":
                    val0 = val1_0 & val2_0
                    val1 = val1_1 & val2_1
                elif op == "OR":
                    val0 = val1_0 | val2_0
                    val1 = val1_1 | val2_1
                elif op == "XOR":
                    val0 = val1_0 ^ val2_0
                    val1 = val1_1 ^ val2_1
                
                values_at_0[var_name] = val0
                values_at_1[var_name] = val1
        
        # Calculate total sum when peter's bit is 0 and 1
        sum_0 = sum(values_at_0[var_name] for var_name in variables.keys())
        sum_1 = sum(values_at_1[var_name] for var_name in variables.keys())
        
        # For minimum sum, choose 0 if sum_0 <= sum_1, else 1
        # For maximum sum, choose 1 if sum_1 >= sum_0, else 0
        if sum_0 <= sum_1:
            min_peter.append('0')
            max_peter.append('1')
        else:
            min_peter.append('1')
            max_peter.append('0')
    
    # Convert to m-bit binary numbers
    min_result = ''.join(min_peter)
    max_result = ''.join(max_peter)
    
    print(min_result)
    print(max_result)
```

Problem

View Problem Statement 
You are an expert Python programmer. You will be given a question (problem specification) and will generate a correct Python program that matches the specification and passes all tests.

Bob recently read about bitwise operations used in computers: AND, OR and XOR. He have studied their properties and invented a new game.

Initially, Bob chooses integer m, bit depth of the game, which means that all numbers in the game will consist of m bits. Then he asks Peter to choose some m-bit number. After that, Bob computes the values of n variables. Each variable is assigned either a constant m-bit number or result of bitwise operation. Operands of the operation may be either variables defined before, or the number, chosen by Peter. After that, Peter's score equals to the sum of all variable values.

Bob wants to know, what number Peter needs to choose to get the minimum possible score, and what number he needs to choose to get the maximum possible score. In both cases, if there are several ways to get the same score, find the minimum number, which he can choose.


-----Input-----

The first line contains two integers n and m, the number of variables and bit depth, respectively (1 ≤ n ≤ 5000; 1 ≤ m ≤ 1000). 

The following n lines contain descriptions of the variables. Each line describes exactly one variable. Description has the following format: name of a new variable, space, sign ":=", space, followed by one of:  Binary number of exactly m bits.  The first operand, space, bitwise operation ("AND", "OR" or "XOR"), space, the second operand. Each operand is either the name of variable defined before or symbol '?', indicating the number chosen by Peter.  

Variable names are strings consisting of lowercase Latin letters with length at most 10. All variable names are different.


-----Output-----

In the first line output the minimum number that should be chosen by Peter, to make the sum of all variable values minimum possible, in the second line output the minimum number that should be chosen by Peter, to make the sum of all variable values maximum possible. Both numbers should be printed as m-bit binary numbers.


-----Examples-----
Input
3 3
a := 101
b := 011
c := ? XOR b

Output
011
100

Input
5 1
a := 1
bb := 0
cx := ? OR a
d := ? XOR ?
e := d AND bb

Output
0
0



-----Note-----

In the first sample if Peter chooses a number 011_2, then a = 101_2, b = 011_2, c = 000_2, the sum of their values is 8. If he chooses the number 100_2, then a = 101_2, b = 011_2, c = 111_2, the sum of their values is 15.

For the second test, the minimum and maximum sum of variables a, bb, cx, d and e is 2, and this sum doesn't depend on the number chosen by Peter, so the minimum Peter can choose is 0.### Format: Read the inputs from stdin solve the problem and write the answer to stdout (do not directly test on the sample inputs). Enclose your code within delimiters as follows. Ensure that when the python program runs, it reads the inputs, runs the algorithm and writes output to STDOUT.

Model Response

View Full Response 
I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We have `n` variables and `m` bit depth
2. Peter chooses an `m`-bit number (represented by '?')
3. Each variable is either:
   - A constant m-bit binary number
   - A bitwise operation (AND, OR, XOR) between previously defined variables or Peter's chosen number
4. Peter's score is the sum of all variable values
5. We need to find:
   - The minimum number Peter should choose to minimize the total score
   - The minimum number Peter should choose to maximize the total score

## Key Insights

1. Since we're dealing with bitwise operations and we want to minimize/maximize the sum, we should consider each bit position independently due to the linearity of bit contributions.

2. For each bit position (0 to m-1), we can determine whether setting that bit to 0 or 1 in Peter's number leads to a lower or higher total sum.

3. Since each bit contributes independently to the sum (2^(bit_position) to the total), we can analyze each bit position separately and then combine the results.

4. For each bit position, we need to calculate the contribution to the total sum when Peter's bit is 0 vs 1.

## Algorithm

1. Parse the input to build a dependency graph of variables
2. For each bit position from 0 to m-1:
   - Calculate the total sum contribution when Peter's bit is 0
   - Calculate the total sum contribution when Peter's bit is 1
   - Choose the bit that gives the minimum sum (for minimum score) or maximum sum (for maximum score)
3. Combine the bits to form the final answer

## Implementation Details

For each bit position, we need to:
1. Simulate what happens to each variable when Peter's bit is 0 or 1
2. Calculate the contribution of each variable to the total sum
3. Compare the total contributions

Since bitwise operations are independent across bit positions, we can process each bit independently.

```python
def solve():
    n, m = map(int, input().split())
    
    variables = {}  # variable name -> (expression, bit_index_mapping)
    expressions = []
    
    for _ in range(n):
        line = input().strip()
        parts = line.split(" := ")
        var_name = parts[0]
        expr = parts[1]
        
        # Parse the expression
        # Format: "101" or "a AND b" or "? AND b"
        if expr[0].isdigit():
            # It's a constant
            constant = expr
        else:
            # It's an operation
            op_parts = expr.split(" ")
            if len(op_parts) == 3:
                left = op_parts[0]
                op = op_parts[1]
                right = op_parts[2]
            else:
                # This shouldn't happen based on the problem description
                left = op_parts[0]
                op = op_parts[1]
                right = op_parts[2] if len(op_parts) > 2 else "?"
            
            constant = None
            expr_parts = [left, op, right]
        
        expressions.append((var_name, expr))
    
    # For each bit position, determine the optimal value
    min_result = ""
    max_result = ""
    
    # Process each bit position from most significant to least
    for bit_pos in range(m):
        # For this bit position, we'll calculate the contribution when Peter's bit is 0 and 1
        total_sum_0 = 0  # when Peter's bit is 0
        total_sum_1 = 0  # when Peter's bit is 1
        
        # We need to simulate what each variable becomes at this bit position
        # But we need to track how each variable's value depends on Peter's bit
        
        # First, let's parse all expressions to understand the dependencies
        # We'll work with the bit positions directly
        
        # Initialize variable values for this bit position
        # We'll simulate all variables in order
        var_values = {}  # variable name -> bit value (0 or 1)
        
        # For each variable in order, compute its value at this bit position
        # In this bit position, Peter's number bit is either 0 or 1
        # We need to simulate both cases
        
        # Case 1: Peter's bit is 0
        var_values_0 = {}
        var_values_1 = {}
        
        # Initialize all variables
        for var_name, expr in expressions:
            # Determine the value of this variable at bit position bit_pos when Peter's bit is 0
            var_values_0[var_name] = 0
            
            # If the expression is a constant, get its bit at position bit_pos
            if expr[0].isdigit():
                # Get the bit of the constant at position bit_pos
                constant_str = expr
                if len(constant_str) > bit_pos + 1:
                    bit_val = int(constant_str[bit_pos])
                else:
                    bit_val = 0
                var_values_0[var_name] = bit_val
            else:
                # It's an operation
                op_parts = expr.split(" ")
                left = op_parts[0]
                op = op_parts[1]
                right = op_parts[2]
                
                # Get values from left and right operands
                left_val = var_values_0[left] if left in var_values_0 else 0
                right_val = var_values_0[right] if right in var_values_0 else 0
                
                # If right is '?', it means Peter's bit
                if right == '?':
                    right_val = 0
                
                # Apply the operation
                if op == "AND":
                    var_values_0[var_name] = left_val & right_val
                elif op == "OR":
                    var_values_0[var_name] = left_val | right_val
                elif op == "XOR":
                    var_values_0[var_name] = left_val ^ right_val
        
        # Case 2: Peter's bit is 1
        var_values_1 = {}
        
        for var_name, expr in expressions:
            if expr[0].isdigit():
                constant_str = expr
                # Get the bit of the constant at position bit_pos
                if len(constant_str) > bit_pos:
                    bit_val = int(constant_str[bit_pos])
                else:
                    bit_val = 0
                var_values_1[var_name] = bit_val
            else:
                op_parts = expr.split(" ")
                left = op_parts[0]
                op = op_parts[1]
                right = op_parts[2]
                
                left_val = var_values_1[left] if left in var_values_1 else 0
                right_val = var_values_1[right] if right in var_values_1 else 0
                
                if right == '?':
                    right_val = 1
                
                if op == "AND":
                    var_values_1[var_name] = left_val & right_val
                elif op == "OR":
                    var_values_1[var_name] = left_val | right_val
                elif op == "XOR":
                    var_values_1[var_name] = left_val ^ right_val
        
        # Calculate total sum for both cases
        total_sum_0 = sum(var_values_0[var_name] * (2 ** bit_pos) for var_name in var_values_0)
        total_sum_1 = sum(var_values_1[var_name] * (2 ** bit_pos) for var_name in var_values_1)
        
        # For minimum score, choose the smaller sum
        if total_sum_0 <= total_sum_1:
            min_result += "0"
        else:
            min_result += "1"
            
        # For maximum score, choose the larger sum
        if total_sum_0 >= total_sum_1:
            max_result += "1"
        else:
            max_result += "0"
    
    print(min_result)
    print(max_result)

solve()
```

```python
            right = parts[2]

    var_values_1 = {}

        if expr[0].isdigit():
            if len(expr) > bit_pos:

            else:

            var_values_1[var_name] = bit_val

            op = parts[1]
            right = parts[2]

                var_values_1[var_name] = left_val | right_val
            elif op == "XOR":
                var_values_1[var_name] = left_val ^ right_val
    
    # Calculate total sum contribution from this bit position
    # Each variable contributes (value) * (2^bit_pos)
    total_sum_0 += sum(var_values_0[var_name] * (2 ** bit_pos) for var_name in var_values_0)
    total_sum_1 += sum(var_values_1[var_name] * (2 ** bit_pos) for var_name in var_values_1)
    
    # For minimum score, choose bit that gives smaller sum
    if total_sum_0 <= total_sum_1:
        min_result += "0"

        min_result += "1"

        max_result += "1"
```

            right = parts[2]
            
            # Get values of operands
            left_val = var_values_0[left] if left in var_values_0 else 0
            right_val = 0
            
            # If right is '?', treat as Peter's bit
            if right == '?':
                right_val = 0
            else:
                # It's a variable name, get its value
                right_val = var_values_0[right] if right in var_values_0 else 0
            
            # Apply operation
            if op == "AND":
                var_values_0[var_name] = left_val & right_val
            elif op == "OR":
                var_values_0[var_name] = left_val | right_val
            elif op == "XOR":
                var_values_0[var_name] = left_val ^ right_val
    
    # Now for Peter's bit = 1
    var_values_1 = {}
    
    for var_name, expr in expressions:
        if expr[0].isdigit():
            if len(expr) > bit_pos:
                bit_val = int(expr[bit_pos])
            else:
                bit_val = 0
            var_values_1[var_name] = bit_val
        else:
            parts = expr.split(" ")
            left = parts[0]
            op = parts[1]
            right = parts[2]
            
            left_val = var_values_1[left] if left in var_values_1 else 0
            right_val = 0
            
            if right == '?':
                right_val = 1
            else:
                right_val = var_values_1[right] if right in var_values_1 else 0
            
            if op == "AND":
                var_values_1[var_name] = left_val & right_val
            elif op == "OR":
                var_values_1[var_name] = left_val | right_val
            elif op == "XOR":
                var_values_1[var_name] = left_val ^ right_val
    
    # Calculate total sum contribution from this bit position
    # Each variable contributes (value) * (2^bit_pos)
    total_sum_0 += sum(var_values_0[var_name] * (2 ** bit_pos) for var_name in var_values_0)
    total_sum_1 += sum(var_values_1[var_name] * (2 ** bit_pos) for var_name in var_values_1)
    
    # For minimum score, choose bit that gives smaller sum
    if total_sum_0 <= total_sum_1:
        min_result += "0"
    else:
        min_result += "1"
    
    # For maximum score, choose bit that gives larger sum
    if total_sum_0 >= total_sum_1:
        max_result += "1"
    else:
        max_result += "0"

print(min_result)
print(max_result)

Problem

View Problem Statement 
You are an expert Python programmer. You will be given a question (problem specification) and will generate a correct Python program that matches the specification and passes all tests.

Bob recently read about bitwise operations used in computers: AND, OR and XOR. He have studied their properties and invented a new game.

Initially, Bob chooses integer m, bit depth of the game, which means that all numbers in the game will consist of m bits. Then he asks Peter to choose some m-bit number. After that, Bob computes the values of n variables. Each variable is assigned either a constant m-bit number or result of bitwise operation. Operands of the operation may be either variables defined before, or the number, chosen by Peter. After that, Peter's score equals to the sum of all variable values.

Bob wants to know, what number Peter needs to choose to get the minimum possible score, and what number he needs to choose to get the maximum possible score. In both cases, if there are several ways to get the same score, find the minimum number, which he can choose.


-----Input-----

The first line contains two integers n and m, the number of variables and bit depth, respectively (1 ≤ n ≤ 5000; 1 ≤ m ≤ 1000). 

The following n lines contain descriptions of the variables. Each line describes exactly one variable. Description has the following format: name of a new variable, space, sign ":=", space, followed by one of:  Binary number of exactly m bits.  The first operand, space, bitwise operation ("AND", "OR" or "XOR"), space, the second operand. Each operand is either the name of variable defined before or symbol '?', indicating the number chosen by Peter.  

Variable names are strings consisting of lowercase Latin letters with length at most 10. All variable names are different.


-----Output-----

In the first line output the minimum number that should be chosen by Peter, to make the sum of all variable values minimum possible, in the second line output the minimum number that should be chosen by Peter, to make the sum of all variable values maximum possible. Both numbers should be printed as m-bit binary numbers.


-----Examples-----
Input
3 3
a := 101
b := 011
c := ? XOR b

Output
011
100

Input
5 1
a := 1
bb := 0
cx := ? OR a
d := ? XOR ?
e := d AND bb

Output
0
0



-----Note-----

In the first sample if Peter chooses a number 011_2, then a = 101_2, b = 011_2, c = 000_2, the sum of their values is 8. If he chooses the number 100_2, then a = 101_2, b = 011_2, c = 111_2, the sum of their values is 15.

For the second test, the minimum and maximum sum of variables a, bb, cx, d and e is 2, and this sum doesn't depend on the number chosen by Peter, so the minimum Peter can choose is 0.### Format: Read the inputs from stdin solve the problem and write the answer to stdout (do not directly test on the sample inputs). Enclose your code within delimiters as follows. Ensure that when the python program runs, it reads the inputs, runs the algorithm and writes output to STDOUT.
```python
# YOUR CODE HERE
```

View Full Response 
I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We have `n` variables and `m` bit depth
2. Peter chooses an m-bit number (represented by '?')
3. Each variable is defined as either:
   - A constant m-bit binary number
   - A bitwise operation (AND, OR, XOR) between previously defined variables or Peter's number ('?')
4. Peter's score is the sum of all variable values
5. We need to find:
   - The minimum number Peter should choose to minimize the total score
   - The minimum number Peter should choose to maximize the total score

## Key Insights

1. Since we're dealing with bitwise operations, we can analyze each bit position independently due to the linearity of bitwise operations over different bit positions.

2. For each bit position (0 to m-1), we can consider whether setting that bit to 0 or 1 gives a lower or higher contribution to the total sum.

3. Since the operations are bitwise, the contribution of each bit position to the total sum is independent of other bit positions.

4. For each bit position, we can calculate the contribution of Peter's choice (0 or 1) to the total sum, and choose the option that minimizes or maximizes the sum.

## Approach

1. Parse the input to build the variable definitions
2. For each bit position, simulate the computation for both Peter choosing 0 and 1 at that position
3. For each bit position, determine which choice (0 or 1) gives the minimum or maximum contribution
4. Combine the results to form the final answer

## Step-by-step Solution

```python
        # and accumulate the count (since each set bit contributes 2^bit_pos)
        
        # But actually, for bit_pos, the value at position bit_pos contributes 2^bit_pos to the sum
```

Wait, I'm making a logical error. The total sum includes all variables' values, not just the bit values. Let me reconsider.

Actually, since we're dealing with bitwise operations and each bit position contributes independently, we can indeed analyze each bit position separately. The total sum is the sum over all bit positions of (2^bit_pos * number of variables with that bit set).

So for each bit position, we calculate the number of variables that have that bit set when Peter chooses 0 vs 1, and choose the option that minimizes or maximizes this count.

```python

                const_val = variables[var_name][2]
                # Extract bit at position bit_pos (bit_pos is 0-indexed from right)
                # The rightmost bit is at position 0, so we need bit_pos-th from right
                # In the string, it's at index (m-1-bit_pos) from left
                index = m - 1 - bit_pos
                return int(const_val[index])
            else:
                # It's an operation

                val1 = get_bit_value(first_op, peter_bit)

                val2 = 0

        bit_mask = 1 << bit_pos
        if val_0 & bit_mask:
            count_set_0 += 1
        if val_1 & bit_mask:
            count_set_1 += 1

    # For maximum sum: choose the option with more set bits
    if count_set_0 <= count_set_1:

        max_peter += '0'
    else:
        min_peter += '1'
        max_peter += '1'

print(min_peter)
```

    count_set_0 = 0
    count_set_1 = 0
    
    for var_name in variables:
        val_0 = get_bit_value(var_name, 0)
        val_1 = get_bit_value(var_name, 1)
        
        # Check if bit_pos bit is set in val_0 and val_1
        bit_mask = 1 << bit_pos
        if val_0 & bit_mask:
            count_set_0 += 1
        if val_1 & bit_mask:
            count_set_1 += 1
    
    # For minimum sum: choose the option with fewer set bits
    # For maximum sum: choose the option with more set bits
    if count_set_0 <= count_set_1:
        min_peter += '0'
        max_peter += '0'
    else:
        min_peter += '1'
        max_peter += '1'

print(min_peter)
print(max_peter)

-----Input-----

I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We have `n` variables and `m` bit depth
2. Each variable is defined as either:
   - A constant m-bit binary number
   - A bitwise operation (AND, OR, XOR) between previous variables or Peter's chosen number (denoted by '?')
3. Peter chooses an m-bit number to minimize/maximize the sum of all variable values
4. If multiple choices give the same score, we want the minimum number

## Key Insights

1. Since we're dealing with bitwise operations, we can analyze each bit position independently
2. For each bit position (0 to m-1), we can determine whether setting that bit to 0 or 1 will minimize/maximize the total sum
3. The operations (AND, OR, XOR) have different behaviors:
   - AND: both operands must be 1 to get 1
   - OR: at least one operand must be 1 to get 1  
   - XOR: gives 1 when operands are different

4. Since we want to optimize the sum bit by bit, and the operations are linear in each bit position, we can solve each bit position independently.

## Algorithm

For each bit position i (0 to m-1):
1. Calculate the contribution of bit i to the total sum for each possible value (0 or 1) of Peter's choice
2. For each position, figure out which value (0 or 1) gives minimum/maximum sum
3. If both values give the same sum, choose 0 (as per requirement)

## Step-by-step Implementation

```python
def solve():
    n, m = map(int, input().split())
    
    # Store variable definitions
    variables = {}  # variable_name -> (expression, bit_position_info)
    
    # Read all variable definitions
    for _ in range(n):
        line = input().strip()
        parts = line.split(" := ")
        var_name = parts[0]
        expr = parts[1]
        
        # Parse the expression
        # Format: operand1 op operand2
        parts = expr.split()
        if len(parts) == 1:
            # Constant
            binary_val = parts[0]
            variables[var_name] = (binary_val, None)
        else:
            # Operation expression
            operand1 = parts[0]
            op = parts[1]
            operand2 = parts[2]
            variables[var_name] = (operand1, op, operand2)
    
    # For each bit position, compute the contribution to total sum
    min_result = []
    max_result = []
    
    for bit_pos in range(m):
        # For each bit position, simulate both 0 and 1 for Peter's choice
        # and compute the sum of all variable bits at this position
        
        # Function to evaluate the bit at position bit_pos for a given Peter's bit
        def get_bit_sum(peter_bit):
            total_bit_sum = 0
            # Evaluate all variables for this bit position
            var_bits = {}  # variable_name -> bit value at this position
            
            # First pass: calculate all variable bits
            for var_name, expr in variables.items():
                if expr[1] is None:  # constant
                    binary_val = expr[0]
                    var_bits[var_name] = int(binary_val[bit_pos])
                else:
                    # It's an expression with operation
                    op1, op, op2 = expr
                    bit1 = var_bits.get(op1, 0)
                    bit2 = var_bits.get(op2, 0)
                    
                    # If any operand is '?', use peter_bit
                    if op1 == '?':
                        bit1 = peter_bit
                    if op2 == '?':
                        bit2 = peter_bit
                    
                    # Process operation
                    if op == 'AND':
                        result_bit = bit1 & bit2
                    elif op == 'OR':
                        result_bit = bit1 | bit2
                    elif op == 'XOR':
                        result_bit = bit1 ^ bit2
                    
                    var_bits[var_name] = result_bit
            
            # Sum all bits at this position
            for bit_val in var_bits.values():
                total_bit_sum += bit_val
            
            return total_bit_sum
        
        # Try both values for Peter's bit
        sum_with_0 = get_bit_sum(0)
        sum_with_1 = get_bit_sum(1)
        
        # Choose the one that minimizes the sum
        if sum_with_0 <= sum_with_1:
            min_result.append('0')
        else:
            min_result.append('1')
        
        # Choose the one that maximizes the sum
        if sum_with_1 >= sum_with_0:
            max_result.append('1')
        else:
            max_result.append('0')
    
    # Convert to m-bit binary strings
    min_num = ''.join(min_result)
    max_num = ''.join(max_result)
    
    print(min_num)
    print(max_num)

solve()
```

### Answer: (use the provided format with backticks)

Model Response

View Full Response 
I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We have n variables and m-bit numbers
2. Peter chooses an m-bit number (represented by '?')
3. Each variable is either:
   - A constant m-bit number
   - A bitwise operation (AND, OR, XOR) between previous variables or Peter's number
4. We need to find:
   - The minimum number Peter should choose to minimize the total sum of all variable values
   - The minimum number Peter should choose to maximize the total sum of all variable values
5. If multiple choices give the same score, we choose the smallest number

## Key Insights

1. Since we're dealing with bitwise operations, we can analyze each bit position independently
2. For each bit position (0 to m-1), we can determine the contribution of that bit to the total sum
3. The total sum is the sum of all variable values, where each variable value contributes based on its bits
4. For each bit position, we can determine whether setting that bit in Peter's number increases or decreases the total sum
5. Since we want the minimum/maximum sum, and if there are ties we want the minimum number, we can build the answer bit by bit

## Approach

1. For each bit position (from most significant to least significant), we'll determine if setting that bit in Peter's number improves the sum
2. We'll simulate the computation of all variables based on Peter's number
3. For each bit position, we'll try 0 and 1 and see which gives better sum
4. We use the fact that bitwise operations can be analyzed independently across bit positions

## Algorithm

1. Parse input and store the variable definitions
2. For each bit position from 0 to m-1:
   - Try setting that bit to 0 and compute the total sum
   - Try setting that bit to 1 and compute the total sum
   - Choose the bit that gives better sum (minimum for min-sum, maximum for max-sum)
   - If sums are equal, choose 0 (since we want minimum number)

The key insight is that bitwise operations are independent across different bit positions. So we can analyze each bit position separately.

```python
def solve():
    n, m = map(int, input().split())
    
    variables = {}
    definitions = []
    
    for _ in range(n):
        line = input().strip()
        parts = line.split(" := ")
        var_name = parts[0]
        expr = parts[1]
        
        # Parse the expression
        if expr[0] == '0' or expr[0] == '1':
            # It's a constant
            expr = expr
        else:
            # It has operands and operator
            # Find the operator
            if 'AND' in expr:
                op_idx = expr.index('AND')
            elif 'OR' in expr:
                op_idx = expr.index('OR')
            elif 'XOR' in expr:
                op_idx = expr.index('XOR')
            
            # Split to get operands
            left_expr = expr[:op_idx].strip()
            op = expr[op_idx:op_idx+3].strip()
            right_expr = expr[op_idx+3:].strip()
            
            # Remove spaces
            left_expr = left_expr.strip()
            right_expr = right_expr.strip()
            
            expr = (left_expr, op, right_expr)
        
        definitions.append((var_name, expr))
    
    # For each bit position, determine the effect on total sum
    min_result = ""
    max_result = ""
    
    # Process each bit position from most significant to least significant
    for bit_pos in range(m-1, -1, -1):
        # We need to compute the total sum for each choice of this bit
        # But we need to simulate the entire computation
        
        # Instead, let's compute for each choice of this bit
        # The actual variable values are computed based on the current settings
        
        def compute_sum(peter_bits):
            # peter_bits is a list of m bits where index 0 is MSB
            # Build the actual value of Peter's number based on bit position
            peter_val = 0
            for i in range(m):
                if peter_bits[i]:
                    peter_val |= (1 << (m-1-i))
            
            # Simulate all variables
            var_values = {}
            
            # Process variables in order
            for var_name, expr in definitions:
                if isinstance(expr, tuple):
                    left_expr, op, right_expr = expr
                    left_val = var_values.get(left_expr, 0)
                    right_val = var_values.get(right_expr, 0)
                    
                    if right_expr == '?':
                        right_val = peter_val
                    elif left_expr == '?':
                        left_val = peter_val
                    elif right_expr == '?':
                        right_val = peter_val
                    elif left_expr == '?':
                        left_val = peter_val
                    
                    if op == "AND":
                        result = left_val & right_val
                    elif op == "OR":
                        result = left_val | right_val
                    elif op == "XOR":
                        result = left_val ^ right_val
                    
                    var_values[var_name] = result
                else:
                    # It's a constant
                    var_values[var_name] = int(expr, 2)
            
            # Sum all variable values
            total = 0
            for var_name, expr in definitions:
                if isinstance(expr, tuple):
                    total += var_values[var_name]
                else:
                    total += int(expr, 2)
            
            return total
        
        # Try two configurations: current bit = 0, current bit = 1
        # We're currently at bit_pos (from most significant to least significant)
        
        # For the current bit position, we'll create two possible configurations
        # First, we need to know what the current bit is in the chosen number
        
        # Try with current bit = 0
        config_0 = list(peter_bits[:]) if peter_bits else []
        config_0[bit_pos] = 0
        
        # Try with current bit = 1
        config_1 = list(peter_bits[:]) if peter_bits else []
        config_1[bit_pos] = 1
        
        # Compute sum for both configurations
        sum_0 = compute_sum(config_0)
        sum_1 = compute_sum(config_1)
        
        # For minimum sum, choose the one with lower sum
        # For maximum sum, choose the one with higher sum
        if sum_0 < sum_1:
            # Choose 0 for minimum sum
            min_result += "0"
            max_result += "0"
        elif sum_0 > sum_1:
            # Choose 1 for minimum sum (if sum_1 is lower)
            min_result += "1"
            max_result += "1"
        else:
            # Equal sums, choose 0 (minimum number)
            min_result += "0"
            max_result += "0"
    
    # The above approach is not correct because we're not properly constructing the bit configurations
    
    # Let me restart with a cleaner approach
    
    def get_variable_value(var_name, peter_bits, variables):
        # peter_bits is a list of m bits (index 0 is MSB)
        # Build Peter's number
        peter_num = 0
        for i in range(m):
            if peter_bits[i] == 1:
                peter_num |= (1 << (m-1-i))
        
        # Create a copy of variables
        var_values = {}
        
        # Process variables in order
        for var_name, expr in definitions:
            if isinstance(expr, tuple):
                left_expr, op, right_expr = expr
                
                left_val = var_values.get(left_expr, 0)
                right_val = var_values.get(right_expr, 0)
                
                if right_expr == '?':
                    right_val = peter_num
                elif left_expr == '?':
                    left_val = peter_num
                
                if op == "AND":
                    result = left_val & right_val
                elif op == "OR":
                    result = left_val | right_val
                elif op == "XOR":
                    result = left_val ^ right_val
                
                var_values[var_name] = result
            else:
                # It's a constant
                var_values[var_name] = int(expr, 2)
        
        return var_values
    
    def compute_total_sum(peter_bits):
        # peter_bits are bits, we need to compute the sum of all variable values
        var_values = get_variable_value(None, peter_bits, definitions)
        
        total = 0
        for var_name, expr in definitions:
            var_val = var_values[var_name]
            total += var_val
        
        return total
    
    # Now we need to find the minimum and maximum sum
    # We'll try all possible combinations of bits? But m can be up to 1000, so we can't try all 2^m
    
    # Instead, we can analyze bit by bit due to independence
    # For each bit position, we can determine if setting that bit in Peter's number helps minimize or maximize the sum
    
    # We'll build the result bit by bit from MSB to LSB
    
    min_bits = [0] * m
    max_bits = [0] * m
    
    for bit_pos in range(m):
        # Try bit_pos in both 0 and 1
        # For each choice, we'll compute the total sum
        # But we need to consider the effect on the entire sum
        
        # Create two configurations
        config_0 = min_bits[:]
        config_1 = min_bits[:]
        
        config_0[bit_pos] = 0
        config_1[bit_pos] = 1
        
        sum_0 = compute_total_sum(config_0)
        sum_1 = compute_total_sum(config_1)
        
        # For minimum sum, choose the bit that gives lower sum
        if sum_0 < sum_1:
            min_bits[bit_pos] = 0
            max_bits[bit_pos] = 0
        elif sum_0 > sum_1:
            min_bits[bit_pos] = 1
            max_bits[bit_pos] = 1
        else:
            # Equal sums, choose 0 (minimum number)
            min_bits[bit_pos] = 0
            max_bits[bit_pos] = 0
    
    # Convert to binary strings
    min_result = ""
    max_result = ""
    
    for bit in min_bits:
        min_result += str(bit)
        max_result += str(bit)
    
    # Reverse to get correct bit order (most significant first)
    min_result = min_result[::-1]
    max_result = max_result[::-1]

def parse_expression(expr):
    # Remove spaces
    expr = expr.replace(" ", "")
    
    if len(expr) == m:
        # It's a constant
        return expr
    
    # Find the operator
    op_idx = -1
    if "AND" in expr:
        op_idx = expr.find("AND")
    elif "OR" in expr:
        op_idx = expr.find("OR")
    elif "XOR" in expr:
        op_idx = expr.find("XOR")
    
    if op_idx == -1:
        return expr
    
    left_part = expr[:op_idx]
    op = expr[op_idx:op_idx+3]
    right_part = expr[op_idx+3:]
    
    return (left_part, op, right_part)

def solve():
    n, m = map(int, input().split())
    
    variables = {}
    definitions = []

        line = input().strip()
        parts = line.split(" := ")
        var_name = parts[0]
        expr = parts[1]
        
        # Parse expression - find the operator
        op_idx = -1
        if "AND" in expr:
            op_idx = expr.find("AND")
        elif "OR" in expr:
            op_idx = expr.find("OR")
        elif "XOR" in expr:
            op_idx = expr.find("XOR")
        
        if op_idx == -1:
            # It's a constant, but ensure it's m bits
            constant = expr.strip()
            if len(constant) != m:
                # Pad with zeros to the left? 
                # Actually, should be exactly m bits
                # Just use as is

            definitions.append((var_name, constant))
        else:
            left_expr = expr[:op_idx].strip()
            op = expr[op_idx:op_idx+3].strip()
            right_expr = expr[op_idx+3:].strip()
            
            definitions.append((var_name, (left_expr, op, right_expr)))
    
    def compute_sum(peter_bits):
        # peter_bits: list of m bits, with index 0 being the MSB
        # Convert to number
        peter_num = 0
        for i in range(m):
            if peter_bits[i] == 1:
                peter_num |= (1 << (m-1-i))
        
        # Simulate all variables
        var_values = {}
        
        for var_name, expr in definitions:
            if isinstance(expr, str):
                # Constant
                var_values[var_name] = int(expr, 2)
            else:
                left_expr, op, right_expr = expr
                
                left_val = var_values.get(left_expr, 0)
                right_val = var_values.get(right_expr, 0)
                
                if right_expr == '?':
                    right_val = peter_num
                elif left_expr == '?':
                    left_val = peter_num
                
                if op == "AND":
                    result = left_val & right_val
                elif op == "OR":
                    result = left_val | right_val
                elif op == "XOR":
                    result = left_val ^ right_val
                
                var_values[var_name] = result
        
        # Sum all variable values
        total_sum = 0
        for var_name, expr in definitions:
            var_val = var_values[var_name]
            total_sum += var_val
        
        return total_sum

    min_bits = [0] * m
    max_bits = [0] * m

    for bit_pos in range(m):
        # Try two configurations: bit_pos = 0 and bit_pos = 1
        config_0 = min_bits[:]
        config_1 = min_bits[:]

        config_1[bit_pos] = 1
```

Wait, I think I have a bug in the bit representation. Let me reconsider.

In the problem, the bit depth is m, and we have m-bit numbers. When we process bit position `bit_pos`, we should consider that it's the `bit_pos`-th bit from the right (LSB is 0), but in the string representation, the leftmost bit is the MSB.

In my solution above, when I create `config_0` and `config_1`, I'm setting `config_0[bit_pos] = 0`, where `bit_pos` is from 0 to m-1.

But I need to think about how the bits are arranged in the number.

Actually, in my `compute_sum` function, when I create `peter_num`, I'm doing:
```python
peter_num |= (1 << (m-1-i))
```

This means that position `i` in the list corresponds to bit `(m-1-i)` in the number.

For example, if m=3 and i=0, it's bit 2 (MSB), i=1 is bit 1, i=2 is bit 0 (LSB).

But when I iterate over `bit_pos` from 0 to m-1, this corresponds to building the number from MSB to LSB.

However, in the `compute_sum` function, when I do `peter_num |= (1 << (m-1-i))`, I'm setting the bit at position `m-1-i` in the number.

But when I set `config_0[bit_pos] = 0`, I'm setting that bit to 0.

The variables are computed correctly because the bit position in the bitwise operations is independent.

Let me verify with the first example:
- a := 101 (constant)
- b := 011 (constant) 
- c := ? XOR b

If Peter chooses 011, then c = 011 XOR 011 = 000, sum = 101 + 011 + 000 = 101 + 3 + 0 = 5 + 3 = 8
If Peter chooses 100, then c = 100 XOR 011 = 111, sum = 101 + 011 + 111 = 5 + 3 + 7 = 15

So 011 gives smaller sum.

In my algorithm, for each bit position:
- Position 0 (MSB): 
  - config_0: Peter's number has MSB 0, so Peter's number is 0?? 
  - config_1: Peter's number has MSB 1, so Peter's number is 1?? 

But I need to consider all bit positions.

Actually, I think my approach is correct because I'm processing each bit position independently and computing the effect on the total sum.

The only potential issue is that the variable values depend on the full value, not just a single bit.

But since bitwise operations are independent across bits, the contribution of each bit position to the total sum is independent.

However, I need to make sure that when I compute the sum for a given configuration, I'm calculating it correctly.

Let me fix the code:

Problem

View Problem Statement 
You are an expert Python programmer. You will be given a question (problem specification) and will generate a correct Python program that matches the specification and passes all tests.

Bob recently read about bitwise operations used in computers: AND, OR and XOR. He have studied their properties and invented a new game.

Initially, Bob chooses integer m, bit depth of the game, which means that all numbers in the game will consist of m bits. Then he asks Peter to choose some m-bit number. After that, Bob computes the values of n variables. Each variable is assigned either a constant m-bit number or result of bitwise operation. Operands of the operation may be either variables defined before, or the number, chosen by Peter. After that, Peter's score equals to the sum of all variable values.

Bob wants to know, what number Peter needs to choose to get the minimum possible score, and what number he needs to choose to get the maximum possible score. In both cases, if there are several ways to get the same score, find the minimum number, which he can choose.


-----Input-----

The first line contains two integers n and m, the number of variables and bit depth, respectively (1 ≤ n ≤ 5000; 1 ≤ m ≤ 1000). 

The following n lines contain descriptions of the variables. Each line describes exactly one variable. Description has the following format: name of a new variable, space, sign ":=", space, followed by one of:  Binary number of exactly m bits.  The first operand, space, bitwise operation ("AND", "OR" or "XOR"), space, the second operand. Each operand is either the name of variable defined before or symbol '?', indicating the number chosen by Peter.  

Variable names are strings consisting of lowercase Latin letters with length at most 10. All variable names are different.


-----Output-----

In the first line output the minimum number that should be chosen by Peter, to make the sum of all variable values minimum possible, in the second line output the minimum number that should be chosen by Peter, to make the sum of all variable values maximum possible. Both numbers should be printed as m-bit binary numbers.


-----Examples-----
Input
3 3
a := 101
b := 011
c := ? XOR b

Output
011
100

Input
5 1
a := 1
bb := 0
cx := ? OR a
d := ? XOR ?
e := d AND bb

Output
0
0



-----Note-----

In the first sample if Peter chooses a number 011_2, then a = 101_2, b = 011_2, c = 000_2, the sum of their values is 8. If he chooses the number 100_2, then a = 101_2, b = 011_2, c = 111_2, the sum of their values is 15.

For the second test, the minimum and maximum sum of variables a, bb, cx, d and e is 2, and this sum doesn't depend on the number chosen by Peter, so the minimum Peter can choose is 0.### Format: Read the inputs from stdin solve the problem and write the answer to stdout (do not directly test on the sample inputs). Enclose your code within delimiters as follows. Ensure that when the python program runs, it reads the inputs, runs the algorithm and writes output to STDOUT.
```python
# YOUR CODE HERE
```

Model Response

View Full Response 
I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We have `n` variables and `m` bit depth
2. Peter chooses an m-bit number (represented by '?')
3. Each variable is defined as either:
   - A constant m-bit binary number
   - A bitwise operation (AND, OR, XOR) between previous variables or Peter's number
4. Peter's score is the sum of all variable values
5. We need to find:
   - The minimum number Peter should choose to minimize the total score
   - The minimum number Peter should choose to maximize the total score
6. If multiple choices give the same score, we pick the minimum number

## Key Insights

1. Since we're dealing with bitwise operations, we can analyze each bit position independently
2. For each bit position (0 to m-1), we can determine how that bit contributes to the total sum
3. The contribution of each bit position depends only on the operations at that position
4. We can evaluate the total score for each possible bit value (0 or 1) at each position

## Approach

1. Parse the input to build the operations
2. For each bit position (0 to m-1):
   - Calculate the contribution of that bit to the total sum when Peter's bit is 0
   - Calculate the contribution when Peter's bit is 1
3. For each position, choose 0 if it gives smaller sum, 1 if it gives larger sum
4. For the minimum score, choose the bit that minimizes the sum at each position
5. For the maximum score, choose the bit that maximizes the sum at each position

## Step-by-step Solution

1. Parse the variable definitions
2. For each bit position, evaluate the contribution to the total sum
3. Since bitwise operations are independent across bit positions, we can analyze each bit separately

```python
def solve():
    n, m = map(int, input().split())
    
    # Store the operations
    variables = {}  # variable name -> (value, operation, operand1, operand2)
    
    for _ in range(n):
        line = input().strip()
        parts = line.split(":=")
        var_name = parts[0].strip()
        rhs = parts[1].strip()
        
        # Parse the right-hand side
        if rhs[0] == '0' or rhs[0] == '1' or (rhs[0] in '01' and len(rhs) == m):
            # This is a constant binary number
            variables[var_name] = (rhs, None, None, None)
        else:
            # Parse operation
            op_parts = rhs.split()
            op = op_parts[1]  # "AND", "OR", "XOR"
            op1 = op_parts[0]
            op2 = op_parts[2]
            variables[var_name] = (None, op, op1, op2)
    
    # For each bit position, calculate the contribution to total sum
    min_result = ""
    max_result = ""
    
    for bit_pos in range(m):
        # We'll evaluate what happens to each variable at this bit position
        # For each variable, we'll determine its value at this bit given Peter's bit
        
        # Function to evaluate a variable's value at a specific bit position
        def get_bit_value(var_name, peter_bit):
            if var_name in variables and variables[var_name][0] is not None:
                # It's a constant
                const_val = variables[var_name][0]
                return int(const_val[bit_pos])
            else:
                # It's an operation
                op = variables[var_name][1]
                op1 = variables[var_name][2]
                op2 = variables[var_name][3]
                
                # Get the values of the operands
                val1 = get_bit_value(op1, peter_bit)
                val2 = get_bit_value(op2, peter_bit)
                
                # Apply the operation
                if op == "AND":
                    return val1 & val2
                elif op == "OR":
                    return val1 | val2
                elif op == "XOR":
                    return val1 ^ val2
        
        # Calculate contribution of this bit position to total sum
        # when Peter's bit is 0
        sum_when_peter_0 = 0
        for var_name in variables:
            val = get_bit_value(var_name, 0)
            sum_when_peter_0 += val
        
        # when Peter's bit is 1
        sum_when_peter_1 = 0
        for var_name in variables:
            val = get_bit_value(var_name, 1)
            sum_when_peter_1 += val
        
        # For minimum score: choose bit that minimizes sum
        if sum_when_peter_0 <= sum_when_peter_1:
            min_result += "0"
        else:
            min_result += "1"
        
        # For maximum score: choose bit that maximizes sum  
        if sum_when_peter_1 >= sum_when_peter_0:
            max_result += "1"

            max_result += "0"
```

```python
def solve():
    n, m = map(int, input().split())
    
    # Store the operations
    variables = {}  # variable name -> (expression type, op, op1, op2)
    # For constants, store the binary string
    # For operations, store (op, op1, op2)
    
    for _ in range(n):
        line = input().strip()
        parts = line.split(":=")
        var_name = parts[0].strip()
        rhs = parts[1].strip()
        
        if len(rhs) == m and rhs[0] in '01':
            # It's a constant
            variables[var_name] = (rhs, None, None, None)
        else:
            # It's an operation
            op_parts = rhs.split()
            op = op_parts[1]
            op1 = op_parts[0]
            op2 = op_parts[2]
            variables[var_name] = (None, op, op1, op2)
    
    # For each bit position, evaluate how the total sum changes with Peter's bit
    min_result = ""
    max_result = ""
    
    for bit_pos in range(m):
        # Evaluate what happens to each variable at this bit position
        # when Peter's bit is 0
        def eval_var(var_name, peter_bit):
            if var_name in variables and variables[var_name][0] is not None:
                # Constant
                s = variables[var_name][0]
                return int(s[bit_pos])
            else:
                # Operation
                op = variables[var_name][1]
                op1 = variables[var_name][2]
                op2 = variables[var_name][3]
                
                # Recursively evaluate the operands
                val1 = eval_var(op1, peter_bit)
                val2 = eval_var(op2, peter_bit)
                
                if op == "AND":
                    return val1 & val2
                elif op == "OR":
                    return val1 | val2
                elif op == "XOR":
                    return val1 ^ val2
        
        # Calculate total sum when Peter's bit is 0
        total_sum_0 = sum(eval_var(var_name, 0) for var_name in variables)
        # Calculate total sum when Peter's bit is 1  
        total_sum_1 = sum(eval_var(var_name, 1) for var_name in variables)
        
        # For minimum score: choose bit that minimizes sum
        if total_sum_0 <= total_sum_1:
            min_result += "0"
        else:
            min_result += "1"
        
        # For maximum score: choose bit that maximizes sum
        if total_sum_1 >= total_sum_0:
            max_result += "1"
        else:
            max_result += "0"
    
    print(min_result)
    print(max_result)
```

Problem

View Problem Statement 
You are an expert Python programmer. You will be given a question (problem specification) and will generate a correct Python program that matches the specification and passes all tests.

Bob recently read about bitwise operations used in computers: AND, OR and XOR. He have studied their properties and invented a new game.

Initially, Bob chooses integer m, bit depth of the game, which means that all numbers in the game will consist of m bits. Then he asks Peter to choose some m-bit number. After that, Bob computes the values of n variables. Each variable is assigned either a constant m-bit number or result of bitwise operation. Operands of the operation may be either variables defined before, or the number, chosen by Peter. After that, Peter's score equals to the sum of all variable values.

Bob wants to know, what number Peter needs to choose to get the minimum possible score, and what number he needs to choose to get the maximum possible score. In both cases, if there are several ways to get the same score, find the minimum number, which he can choose.


-----Input-----

The first line contains two integers n and m, the number of variables and bit depth, respectively (1 ≤ n ≤ 5000; 1 ≤ m ≤ 1000). 

The following n lines contain descriptions of the variables. Each line describes exactly one variable. Description has the following format: name of a new variable, space, sign ":=", space, followed by one of:  Binary number of exactly m bits.  The first operand, space, bitwise operation ("AND", "OR" or "XOR"), space, the second operand. Each operand is either the name of variable defined before or symbol '?', indicating the number chosen by Peter.  

Variable names are strings consisting of lowercase Latin letters with length at most 10. All variable names are different.


-----Output-----

In the first line output the minimum number that should be chosen by Peter, to make the sum of all variable values minimum possible, in the second line output the minimum number that should be chosen by Peter, to make the sum of all variable values maximum possible. Both numbers should be printed as m-bit binary numbers.


-----Examples-----
Input
3 3
a := 101
b := 011
c := ? XOR b

Output
011
100

Input
5 1
a := 1
bb := 0
cx := ? OR a
d := ? XOR ?
e := d AND bb

Output
0
0



-----Note-----

In the first sample if Peter chooses a number 011_2, then a = 101_2, b = 011_2, c = 000_2, the sum of their values is 8. If he chooses the number 100_2, then a = 101_2, b = 011_2, c = 111_2, the sum of their values is 15.

For the second test, the minimum and maximum sum of variables a, bb, cx, d and e is 2, and this sum doesn't depend on the number chosen by Peter, so the minimum Peter can choose is 0.### Format: Read the inputs from stdin solve the problem and write the answer to stdout (do not directly test on the sample inputs). Enclose your code within delimiters as follows. Ensure that when the python program runs, it reads the inputs, runs the algorithm and writes output to STDOUT.
```python
# YOUR CODE HERE
```

I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We have `n` variables and `m` bit depth
2. Peter chooses an m-bit number (represented by '?')
3. Each variable is defined either as a constant or as a bitwise operation between previous variables or Peter's number
4. We need to find the value of Peter's number that minimizes the sum of all variable values, and the value that maximizes it
5. If multiple values give the same score, we return the smallest number

## Key Insights

1. Since we're dealing with bitwise operations and we want to minimize/maximize the sum, we can analyze each bit position independently
2. For each bit position (0 to m-1), we can determine whether setting that bit to 0 or 1 will contribute to the total sum
3. The operations AND, OR, XOR are bit-wise independent, so we can process each bit position separately
4. For each bit position, we evaluate how the value of that bit in Peter's number affects the final sum

## Approach

1. Process each bit position from 0 to m-1
2. For each bit position, determine the contribution of setting that bit to 0 vs 1
3. For each bit position:
   - Calculate what the resulting bit values would be for each variable if Peter's bit is 0
   - Calculate what the resulting bit values would be if Peter's bit is 1
   - Sum up the contributions (bit value * 2^position)
   - Compare the total sums and choose the better option (minimum or maximum)
4. For ties, choose 0 (smallest number)

## Step-by-step Solution

```python
def solve():
    n, m = map(int, input().split())
    
    # Store definitions
    variable_definitions = {}
    
    for _ in range(n):
        line = input().strip()
        parts = line.split(" := ")
        var_name = parts[0]
        expr = parts[1]
        
        # Process the expression
        if ' ' not in expr:
            # Constant
            constant_str = expr
            variable_definitions[var_name] = (constant_str, None)
        else:
            # Operation
            op_parts = expr.split()
            if len(op_parts) == 3:
                op1, op, op2 = op_parts
                variable_definitions[var_name] = (None, (op1, op, op2))
    
    # For each bit position, evaluate the impact of setting Peter's bit
    result_min = []
    result_max = []
    
    for bit_pos in range(m):
        # Function to evaluate a variable's value at this bit position
        def evaluate(var_name, peter_bit):
            if var_name in variable_definitions and variable_definitions[var_name][0] is not None:
                # It's a constant
                constant = variable_definitions[var_name][0]
                # Extract the bit at position bit_pos (0-indexed from left)
                return int(constant[bit_pos])
            else:
                # It's an operation
                op1, op, op2 = variable_definitions[var_name][1]
                bit1 = evaluate(op1, peter_bit)
                bit2 = evaluate(op2, peter_bit)
                
                if op == 'AND':
                    return bit1 & bit2
                elif op == 'OR':
                    return bit1 | bit2
                elif op == 'XOR':
                    return bit1 ^ bit2
        
        # Calculate sum of all variable values when Peter's bit is 0
        sum_0 = 0
        for var_name, _ in variable_definitions.items():
            bit_val = evaluate(var_name, 0)
            sum_0 += bit_val * (2 ** bit_pos)
        
        # Calculate sum when Peter's bit is 1
        sum_1 = 0
        for var_name, _ in variable_definitions.items():
            bit_val = evaluate(var_name, 1)
            sum_1 += bit_val * (2 ** bit_pos)
        
        # Choose the optimal bit for this position
        if sum_0 < sum_1:
            result_min.append('0')
            result_max.append('1')
        elif sum_0 > sum_1:
            result_min.append('1')
            result_max.append('0')
        else:
            # Equal, choose 0
            result_min.append('0')
            result_max.append('0')
    
    min_num = ''.join(result_min)
    max_num = ''.join(result_max)
    
    print(min_num)
    print(max_num)
```

```python
        bit_val = evaluate(var_name, 1)
```

View Problem Statement 
You are an expert Python programmer. You will be given a question (problem specification) and will generate a correct Python program that matches the specification and passes all tests.

Solve the following coding problem using the programming language python:

We have A balls with the string S written on each of them and B balls with the string T written on each of them.
From these balls, Takahashi chooses one with the string U written on it and throws it away.
Find the number of balls with the string S and balls with the string T that we have now.

Constraints

* S, T, and U are strings consisting of lowercase English letters.
* The lengths of S and T are each between 1 and 10 (inclusive).
* S \not= T
* S=U or T=U.
* 1 \leq A,B \leq 10
* A and B are integers.

Input

Input is given from Standard Input in the following format:


S T
A B
U


Output

Print the answer, with space in between.

Examples

Input

red blue
3 4
red


Output

2 4


Input

red blue
5 5
blue


Output

5 4

The input will be given via stdin and the output should be printed to stdout by your code.

Now solve the problem by providing the code.### Format: Read the inputs from stdin solve the problem and write the answer to stdout (do not directly test on the sample inputs).
